# Ryūen Kakeru

*Character · Main · 1-C*

Ryūen Kakeru is named in 32 of 36 volumes. 153 scenes from 32 volumes have been read line by line, with a page for every line.

## Scenes

### Year 1, Volume 2

#### p.241 — Ch.7 sec. 7.4: named before he is seen

- **Caveat** (p.241) — The volume names him for the first time in Ichinose’s warning: "Someone named Ryuuen-kun masterminded that one." — the earlier Class B–Class C dispute, and the Sudou setup behind this case.
- **Trait** (p.241) — As Ichinose describes him: "He doesn’t hesitate to hurt other people for the sake of his own interests. He’s a formidable opponent."
- **Limit** (p.241) — She adds that he "hasn’t seen any reason to reveal himself just yet," which is why Ayanokōji does not know the name.

#### p.254–255 — Ch.7 sec. 7.6: the boy in the corridor

He stops in front of Ayanokōji and Horikita in the corridor — black hair worn long, about Ayanokōji’s height, a wide grin the page calls ominous, never fully turning to face them. The scene does not name him.

- **Spoken** (p.254) — "Setting up a camera? You really did something funny, huh?"
- **Spoken** (p.254) — "Next time, I’ll be your opponent. I look forward to it."
- **What happens** (p.254) — He walks on without answering Horikita’s "And you are?"
- **Position** (p.255) — Ayanokōji’s closing inventory names the encounter: "We’d received a declaration of war from some guy apparently known as Ryuuen."

### Year 1, Volume 3

#### p.109–115 — Ch.3 sec. 3.2: the beach at zero

Holds court on the Class C beach in swimwear — tanned, laid back in his chair, flashing white teeth.

- **Spoken** (p.111) — “Just as you see. We're enjoying our summer vacation.”
- **Spoken** (p.111) — “Hmm. Well, I didn't make a precise calculation,”
- **What happens** (p.111) — Pours his remaining water on the sand “almost in provocation” while Ishizaki fetches another bottle.
- **Spoken** (p.111) — “Class D, the lowest of all, has to put up with starvation, heat, and futility just to save a measly 100 or 200 class points. It makes me laugh.”
- **Spoken** (p.112) — “I said to bring me cold water. This water's warm.”
- **Trait** (p.112) — “Ryuuen's pupils were just like those of a snake.” — the returned bottle comes back in Ishizaki's face.
- **Spoken** (p.112) — “Cooperation? Don't make me laugh. People betray each other with ease. People lie. Relationships built on trust just aren't viable. You can only trust yourself.”
- **Spoken** (p.112) — “I absolutely loathe hard work. Patience? Saving? You must be joking.”
- **Spoken** (p.113) — “Ah. She ran out of here rather suddenly. She went looking for help from another class in the end? Pathetic girl.”
- **Relationship · Ibuki Mio** (p.113) — Admits her light punishment was a slap — he gestures it — and offers forgiveness only on his terms: “If she grovels on the ground, I'll forgive her. I've a tolerant heart.”
- **Spoken** (p.113) — “Another boy defied me as well, so I had him driven out. I heard he didn't die, so he's probably off somewhere eating grass and insects to survive.”
- **Tactic** (p.113) — Spent the entire three-hundred-point budget on day one — “Yes, as you said. I used all of our points.” — so there is nothing left to penalize.
- **Spoken** (p.114) — “Ha ha ha. What should I do, I wonder? Well, I suppose that plain, ordinary people can only engage in plain, ordinary thinking.”
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.114) — Calls her by her first name against her refusal, and answers her disgust while touching himself under the bathing suit.
- **Spoken** (p.114) — “See you later, Suzune.”
- **Spoken** (p.114) — “Well, I rather like forceful women. I'll make you submit to me eventually. When that time comes, it will be the ultimate pleasure.”
- **Relationship · Ishizaki Daichi** (p.111–112) — Treats his fetch-boy as furniture — the warm bottle is thrown back at him.

#### p.221–224 — Ch.6 sec. 6.9: collecting the card

Answers Ibuki’s flashlight signal in the wet woods to collect what his operation stole.

- **Relationship · Ibuki Mio** (p.221) — His spy since the test began — she reports to him over the transceiver and hands the card over.
- **Spoken** (p.221) — “Yo. Excellent work, Ibuki. You did well.”
- **Spoken** (p.221) — “Naturally? If you hadn’t made any mistakes until now, I wouldn’t have had to risk coming over here.”
- **Spoken** (p.222) — “So, where’s the card?”
- **What happens** (p.222) — Checks the engraved name under his flashlight and calls Katsuragi out of the shadows to confirm it.
- **Spoken** (p.222) — “You come over here and confirm it, too. That was your condition, remember? Relax, it’s dark out, and this weather is horrible. There shouldn’t be anyone here. It’s great to be cautious, but don’t waste time.”
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.222) — Holds her stolen leader card under the flashlight, engraved name up.
- **Spoken** (p.223) — “Are you convinced now?”
- **Caveat** (p.223) — Untagged; it follows the card and the inspection, so it is recorded as his.
- **Spoken** (p.223) — “Don’t sit there and think about it forever. The situation is black-and-white, so make your decision. You’re already halfway there. Don’t do something as stupid as pulling out here.”
- **Tactic** (p.223) — Presses the offer with Sakayanagi’s faction rumor — the student-council failure — framed as Katsuragi’s chance.
- **Spoken** (p.223) — “If this wasn’t an honorable deed, then what are you going to do? Did you know that Sakayanagi’s faction has dominated ever since rumors spread that you failed to get on the student council despite your best efforts? This might be your chance, right?”
- **Spoken** (p.223) — “Class A maintains its position by forming alliances. If you can form those, even those who have double-crossed you will return under your wing, won’t they? Or you could make me your enemy, I suppose? If you do, I wonder what would happen?”
- **Spoken** (p.223) — “That’s good. You’ve exercised sound judgment.”
- **Spoken** (p.223) — “To form an alliance. With Class A.”
- **Relationship · Katsuragi Kōhei** (p.223) — Takes his handshake on the Class A alliance after the pressure — the narration calls it a contract with the devil.
- **Spoken** (p.224) — “I have that covered, of course. He will have no choice but to honor his promise.”
- **Caveat** (p.224) — The answer to Ibuki’s renege question is untagged; it is his, against her doubt.
- **What happens** (p.224) — Lays out the contract’s terms to Ibuki with a creepy smile — the page never quotes them.

#### p.241–257 — Ch.7: the lone Class C

The only Class C student left on the beach comes over to provoke the Class D trio, hears the results in his face, and later turns up in the lounge retelling as the variable with the most information.

- **Spoken** (p.241) — “Oh, hey there, sycophants. What happened to Suzune?”
- **Spoken** (p.241) — “I know you’ve been chasing Suzune’s ass. We were together before, you know.”
- **What happens** (p.241–242) — Drains his cup, then crushes it and throws it at Ayanokōji’s feet.
- **Spoken** (p.242) — “Throw that away for me.”
- **Spoken · Sudō Ken** (p.242) — “What a stupid thing to say. Huh? Pick up your own trash, jerk.”
- **Spoken** (p.242) — “But picking up garbage seems like the perfect task for garbage.”
- **Spoken · Hirata Yōsuke** (p.242) — “Calm down, Sudou-kun. Here, I’ll throw it away.”
- **Spoken** (p.242) — “Who are you? More importantly, where’s Suzune? I’ve dreamed of squeezing her bottom.”
- **Spoken · Sudō Ken** (p.242) — “The next time you say something stupid, I’ll kill you,” Sudou growled.
- **Caveat** (p.242) — “What are you doing?” and “Huh? What’s with you? Getting all excited by yourself, huh?” run untagged around Sudou’s tagged threat and are left out as chapter attribution gaps.
- **Spoken · Hirata Yōsuke** (p.242) — “Horikita-san retired yesterday. She’s not here.”
- **Spoken** (p.242) — “Retired? Suzune? She doesn’t seem like that kind of girl.”
- **Caveat** (p.242) — “I thought you retired, Ryuuen-kun.” and the interrupted reply “That’s¾” are left out as chapter attribution gaps; the broken three-quarter mark is kept as printed.
- **Spoken · Sudō Ken** (p.243) — “That’s what we should be saying to you Class C people. You used up all of your points, right? Don’t make us laugh.”
- **Spoken** (p.243) — “Ha. I’m jealous of the nerve of you small fry. How you can be satisfied with that amount of points…”
- **Spoken** (p.244) — “That’s right. I wrote it on the paper, didn’t I? The name of Class D’s leader.”
- **Spoken** (p.244) — “Also, those guys in A and B wrote the same thing, too. Do you know what this means?”
- **Rule** (p.244) — The page’s own reading of the reveal: if the name holds, Class D is hit with penalties and loses 100 points.
- **Caveat** (p.244) — “It doesn’t really matter whatever you say; Class C is still going to stay at zero points.” runs untagged between Ryuuen’s taunts and is left out as a chapter attribution gap.
- **Spoken** (p.244) — “Zero?”
- **What happens** (p.245) — Can only watch in silence as the Class D trio heads for the boat with Sudou’s gesture in the air.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.253) — Read as the variable with more information than himself — the identities of all the leaders in hand.
- **Fact** (p.256) — Classes A and C were connected behind the scenes: Class C sacrificed its own points and bought whatever Class A needed, and by taking all of C’s tools Class A spent the week without using any points — Ibuki’s evidence went to someone in Class A.
- **Fact** (p.256) — Ibuki hiding a wireless transceiver in the ground proved someone was left on the island for her to contact; Ryuuen set it on a table unguarded while on vacation — no one else controlled it, and his mistake was trusting no one.
- **Fact** (p.257) — The test in one line: Class A failed on its internal rift, Class B ran defense-only and paid for believing Kaneda, Class C sent spies and negotiated with A, and Class D avoided damage because he had been appointed the leader.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.257) — Named the number one enemy — spies for the leaders’ identities plus whatever the Class A negotiations bought.

### Year 1, Volume 4

#### p.57–60 — Ch.2 sec. 2.5: the walk-through provocation

Arrives uninvited at the gathering with three terrified lackeys, sizes up the pond, and picks a fight with Katsuragi.

- **Spoken** (p.57) — “Well, well. So many fish in this pond. I’ve come to take a look for myself.”
- **Spoken** (p.58) — “Unfortunately, it looks like I’m with you.” (to Katsuragi’s question whether he belongs to the meeting)
- **Spoken** (p.58) — “How about you put on a little show for me? What do you think of Beauty and the Beast?” (looking at both Horikita and Katsuragi, then letting out a demented little chuckle)
- **Spoken** (p.58) — “Academic ability? How droll. That kind of thing is meaningless.”
- **Spoken** (p.58) — “Hmm? Awful attitude? What in the world are you talking about? I can’t possibly imagine. Can you perhaps tell me how I am, in fact, awful?”
- **What happens** (p.58) — Gestured to his crew as if to ask whether Katsuragi’s argument was believable; “their response mechanical.”
- **Trait** (p.58) — His three followers wear terrified looks and move docile — “more like a king and his servants.”
- **Relationship · Katsuragi Kōhei** (p.57–58) — The academic-society argument ends in a cold stalemate.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.60) — She flips her hair and turns her back on him to go inside.

#### p.64–65, p.67 — Ch.3: the Dragon roster and the wariness answer

The Dragon roster prints his name as the Class C seat of Horikita’s group.

- **Caveat** (p.64) — Listed as Ryuuen Kakeru in Class C with Oda Takumi, Suzuki Hidetoshi, and Sonoda Masashi; the Class D line of the same group is Kushida, Hirata, and Horikita.
- **Caveat** (p.65) — The group read: Horikita’s inclusion isn’t necessarily advantageous — she and Ryuuen are especially incompatible, like fire and water.
- **Caveat** (p.67) — Asked who he’s most wary of, Ayanokōji answers Ryuuen, by a mile — after naming Katsuragi the most excellent.

#### p.68–75 — Ch.3 sec. 3.0: the café provocation

Walks into Blue Ocean with Ibuki mid-Horikita-briefing, straddles a vacant seat, and spends the coffee working the island result until he leaves with his lackey.

- **Spoken** (p.68) — “Nice weather, eh Suzune? You having breakfast with this clingy beta male?”
- **Spoken** (p.68) — “You should have received the message already. What were the results? Were you chosen as the VIP?”
- **Spoken** (p.69) — “I suppose you wouldn’t give anything away, but that doesn’t matter. According to my information, there’s no way that your farcical bumbling on the island should’ve led to victory,” said Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.70) — “Yesterday, it seemed Katsuragi was rather cautious of you,” he said.
- **Spoken** (p.70) — “Heh. Well, I won’t deny that. I came here to measure your abilities for myself,” he answered.
- **Spoken** (p.72) — “Katsuragi and I think differently. When I’m dealing with someone, I like to keep an eye on them.”
- **Spoken** (p.72) — “I’m remembering the test on the island. The result. The process that led to it. There are only certain types of people who would’ve been able to conceptualize and execute a plan like that. A girl like you is far too serious-minded to have come up with it,” reasoned Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.72) — “Katsuragi probably didn’t know.” (his read on who grasped the points flow)
- **Spoken** (p.72) — “At the end of the test, I wrote down your name as leader, but that was wrong. There’s just one reason why that was wrong: Your class leader changed right before the test ended. The only explanation,” mused Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.72) — “Ah. However, Katsuragi thinks you planned the whole thing. But was that really the case? In my opinion, you becoming the leader and then retiring were both unexpected. Besides, I had my own strategy in place. I had Ibuki infiltrate your class to find out the leader’s identity. You did nothing to counter that strategy at first,” said Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.73) — “The key question is who replaced you as leader. Personally, I think the second leader was pulling your strings from behind the scenes during the entire test.”
- **Trait** (p.73) — Speaks to Horikita but watches Ayanokōji the whole time — “If I appeared shaken for even an instant, he’d pounce.”
- **Spoken** (p.73) — “Well, I suppose that’s expected of a clingy tagalong.”
- **Spoken** (p.73) — “That guy with you is considerably smart. Despite that, he hasn’t achieved anything significant, or gotten high grades. He has some excellent qualities, though I have my doubts,” said Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.74) — “I see your point. I never imagined you’d trip me up. I’ll admit it. The results of the test shocked me,” answered Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.74) — “Well, that’s too bad. I love surprise attacks, foul play, and subterfuge. That level of strategy was totally unexpected, but your victory high will soon come to an end. Whether it’s you, Suzune, or someone pulling the strings behind the scenes, you’re stupid. You’ve already made your best move. Class D is one or two steps behind the other classes in points. The challenge will only heat up from here. You already played your trump card during the survival test, when the game was just getting started. You couldn’t see what was in front of or behind you. I don’t think you’ll find this round as easy as last time. Tell that to whoever helped you ace the test,” warned Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.74) — “What can I say? I’m quite merciful.”
- **What happens** (p.74) — Snaps a photo of Horikita without asking, studies the sour expression, and pockets the phone satisfied.
- **Spoken** (p.74) — “Someone in Class D other than you is quite clever. There’s no mistake about that,” he said.
- **Spoken** (p.75) — “A conversation reveals many subtle things. I’m glad I could talk with you, Suzune. It’s a game to me. I’ll discover who among you is making moves behind the scenes. Everyone, including your clingy friend here, is a target,” said Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.75) — “I already know their abilities, to some extent. Neither Katsuragi nor Ichinose is my enemy. If I wanted to crush them, I could do it any time.”
- **Spoken** (p.75) — “I’m saving her for the final course. It’d be a waste to eat her now. Let’s go, Ibuki.” Ryuuen stood and left with his lackey. (on Sakayanagi)
- **Trait** (p.68–75) — The whole visit is reconnaissance: measure Horikita, float the hidden-second-leader theory, and leave without confirming anything — narration says he only wanted to chat and pass the time amusing himself.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.68–75) — The target of the visit; he needles her, photographs her, and walks off still doubting her.
- **Relationship · Ibuki Mio** (p.68–75) — Brings her as the visible lackey and cuts her off when she would snap back.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.68, 73–75) — Sizes him up as smart-but-inert and keeps him in the target set without ever addressing him directly.
- **Relationship · Katsuragi Kōhei** (p.70, 72, 75) — The rival whose caution and island read he quotes back at Horikita.
- **Relationship · Sakayanagi Arisu** (p.75) — Saved “for the final course” — he will not spend her now.

#### p.76, p.84 — Ch.3: the strategy in motion and the talent bar

Named as the man already moving his new strategy after the café interrupt.

- **Caveat** (p.76) — Ibuki’s walk with Ryuuen reads as his new strategy already being implemented — his showing up here is called dangerous.
- **Caveat** (p.84) — In the talent comparison, not even Katsuragi or Ryuuen could match Ichinose’s de facto leadership.

#### p.115, p.123 — Ch.3: named twice, absent both times

Two second-half mentions — the Kouenji shock list and the teachers’ Dragon roster talk.

- **Position** (p.115) — “Katsuragi, Ryuuen, and Ichinose would all be surprised by this” — grouped with them as a leader who had not imagined an early traitor.
- **Position** (p.122-123) — Hoshinomiya’s Class C complaint: “If we evaluated the Class … C students properly, another student should’ve been placed into Dragon. But they threw Ryuuen in there.”
- **Relationship · Hoshinomiya Chie** (p.123) — The teacher who thinks the Dragon pick was a Class C evaluation failure.
- **Relationship · Katsuragi Kōhei** (p.115) — The other surprised leader on the same line.
- **Relationship · Ichinose Honami** (p.115) — The third name on the surprise list.

#### p.128–131 — Ch.4 sec. 4.0: the shade meeting and the planted phone

He drops into Horikita and Ayanokōji’s shade conversation, claims Class C can win by rule-logic, and leaves a silent recording phone under his own chair.

- **Spoken** (p.128) — “Hey, you two. Having yourselves a nice date in the shade? Let me in on the fun.”
- **Spoken** (p.128) — “That’s for me to decide. Well then, have you decided how to find the VIP?” he asked.
- **Spoken** (p.128) — “I’ve already started to discover the VIP’s identity. Would you believe me if I told you that?”
- **Spoken** (p.128) — “Well, you’re right that I’m not a professional friend-collector, but making friends has nothing to do with whether or not I can gather information.”
- **Spoken** (p.128) — “Unfortunately for you, I’ve already gotten a handle on the fundamentals of this test. Depending on how things go, Class C will win by an overwhelming majority.”
- **Spoken** (p.128-129) — “It’s an extremely simple matter. All you need to do is find out what … class the VIP is from. Then, your next move is to analyze the group,” said Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.129) — “You just have to make sure it’s a situation where someone can’t lie,” he said.
- **Spoken** (p.129) — “I take everyone’s cell phones. So if someone lies to me, all I have to do is check everyone’s email, one by one,” said Ryuuen.
- **Spoken** (p.129) — “It’s not really a problem at all, actually. I’m here because it’s not a problem. Do you get what I mean?”
- **Feat** (p.128-130) — Brute-force phone seizure as a Class C method — no complaints means consent; narration reads it as operating within the rules and as three VIPs already identified.
- **Spoken** (p.129) — “Looks like you finally understand.”
- **Spoken** (p.130) — “Maybe I’m just playing around?”
- **Spoken** (p.130) — “Now, then. Suppose I’m about to reach checkmate?” he said.
- **Spoken** (p.130) — “I don’t think about it at all. I don’t really care about small fry. See you again, Suzune.”
- **Relationship · Kōenji Rokusuke** (p.130) — Dismisses the Monkey-group early end as small fry.
- **What happens** (p.130) — Leaves; a silent phone set to record audio stays under his chair with the chat line “Sorry for yesterday!”
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.130) — Parting “See you again, Suzune” — first-name basis, reported.

#### p.132, p.139–150, p.153, p.159 — Ch.4: the three-way deadlock

Named in the teachers’-conversation echo that pins the group’s academic shape — then stays in the Dragon room as Ichinose and Ayanokōji wait outside.

- **Caveat** (p.132) — The overheard teacher talk and what Ryuuen had said leave no doubt about the pure-academic ranking of the room.
- **Caveat** (p.139) — The narration contrasts the respectful Class B table with the type of relationship Katsuragi and Ryuuen have with their subjects.
- **Caveat** (p.140) — Ichinose can’t imagine Ryuuen and Katsuragi holding a discussion — maybe Class B is showing off their power.
- **Caveat** (p.143) — All of the Class C students leave the room except Ryuuen.
- **Caveat** (p.144) — Kushida reports they’re still discussing things with Ryuuen-kun; inside, Horikita, Kanzaki, and Ryuuen sit slightly apart — a three-way deadlock.
- **Caveat** (p.145) — Ryuuen needles Ayanokouji on sniffing around chicks — first Suzune, now Ichinose — and presses Ichinose on landing in the losers’ group.
- **Caveat** (p.146) — He floats the proposal to crush Class A, claims to already know all the Class C VIP identities, and the narration calls it a very Ryuuen-like plan.
- **Caveat** (p.147) — Even if Suzune wanted to agree, there’s a reason she can’t — he’s willing to leave Classes A and D stripped bare.
- **Caveat** (p.148) — The Ichinose–Ryuuen team-up reads as extremely dangerous; he mocks Ayanokouji as tagalong and gets his honest opinion back.

### Year 1, Volume 7

#### p.11–13 — Ch.1: Ryuuen Kakeru’s Soliloquy

First-person childhood monologue: field-trip snake, the rush of an enemy’s fall, and the boredom of having no one left to overthrow.

- **Inner voice** (p.11) — “I learned something about myself that day.”
- **Inner voice** (p.11) — “Fear and pleasure are two sides of the same coin to me; violence rules the world, and the effectiveness of your own particular brand of violence determines what you accomplish.”
- **Inner voice** (p.11) — “Those who possess an unparalleled capacity for violence are truly the elite.”
- **Inner voice** (p.11) — “There was just one problem, and it grew as I did. It became increasingly difficult for me to feel pleasure.”
- **Inner voice** (p.11) — “I was bored. Bored because no one could fulfill my dearest wish—to be overthrown.”
- **Inner voice** (p.12) — “Perhaps I’d only meet my match in death.”
- **Trait** (p.11–12) — Abnormal from elementary school: kills a snake without fear; enemies give adrenaline; revenge and reversal as the only projects; elite defined by capacity for violence; pleasure fades without an equal.
- **Caveat** (p.10) — ToC leaf before the chapter; no story content.
- **Caveat** (p.13) — Blank page (page number only).

Chooses entertainment over a draw, orders the door shut, and starts the brawl he cannot win.

### Year 1, Volume 10

#### p.30–84 — Ch.2: The Class Vote

Ryūen reads a book alone in Class D; Ayanokōji Group observes him. Horikita visits Ryūen; he claims he could survive if he wanted. Ryūen sees through Horikita’s weakness; says her class made her complacent. Ryūen reveals he accepted his defeat by Ishizaki; Horikita provokes him. Ryūen says Horikita is bluffing about steeling her resolve. Horikita says Ryūen is doomed; Ayanokōji agrees.

- **Spoken** (p.61) — “I make for a good target for the whole lot.”
- **Caveat** (p.72) — Ryūen tells Horikita the class has made her complacent and she could not be ruthless enough to choose; he says she would have had to avoid lingering attachments from the start or treat classmates like chess pieces like Sakayanagi (pp.72, 78).
- **Spoken** (p.68) — “It’s Ishizaki’s reward as the one who defeated me, so I plan on sitting back and accepting it.”
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.58–74) — Horikita visits him; he sees through her weakness.
- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.65) — Says her class made her complacent.
- **What happens** (p.63) — Sits alone in the cafe reading a book borrowed from the library.
- **Spoken** (p.73) — “I was wondering who it was, but it was just Suzune and her mindless little follower.”
- **What happens** (p.73) — Closes the book and faces Horikita.
- **Spoken** (p.73) — “And what sort of business do you have with me?”
- **Spoken** (p.73) — “It’s not like I have a lot of choices. I’m not going to do anything.”
- **Spoken** (p.73) — “I make for a good target for the whole lot. In an exam like this one where someone has to be expelled, nobody wants to attract the resentment of the poor sap who gets kicked out, but in my case, that just ain’t how it works.”
- **What happens** (p.73) — Reopens his book and continues reading.
- **Spoken** (p.74) — “That’s probably right. Class D doesn’t stand a chance after I’m gone. As students from the other classes, it would be the best and most appropriate judgment for you to crush me here.”
- **Spoken** (p.74) — “Kuku. Certainly.”
- **Spoken** (p.75) — “What a stupid question. Of course I could.”
- **Spoken** (p.76) — “Are you finally satisfied? Or do you want to hear my secret plan for surviving the exam?”
- **Spoken** (p.76) — “Your struggling may lead you to being ostracised by the rest of your class.”
- **Spoken** (p.77) — “How lame. So it was all just a bluff?”
- **Spoken** (p.77) — “You’re looking for self-confidence from talking to me, but your resolve doesn’t amount to anything.”
- **Spoken** (p.77) — “No you can’t.”
- **Spoken** (p.77) — “I’ve had plenty of time to keep tabs on you this past year, so I understand you well enough. Putting it in a way even you could understand, I can see the weakness you’re trying to keep hidden.”
- **Spoken** (p.77) — “Your class has already made you complacent.”
- **Caveat** (p.77–78) — Tells Horikita she could not be ruthless enough to make a choice at this point and would have had to avoid lingering attachments or treat classmates like chess pieces like Sakayanagi.
- **Spoken** (p.78) — “And why would you think that?”
- **Spoken** (p.79) — “It’s Ishizaki’s reward as the one who defeated me, so I plan on sitting back and accepting it. It’s an opportunity that you, and the rest of Class D, shouldn’t miss out on.”
- **What happens** (p.79) — Turns his attention back to his book as Horikita and Ayanokōji leave.

#### p.239–246 — Ch.6: Accepting expulsion

Understands Ishizaki is under pressure as the one credited with defeating him and decides not to resist the class’s vote to expel him. Leaves the classroom right after school, not wanting to waste time before the exam ends. Receives a visit from Ibuki; appears from the bath in boxers. Transfers all his private points to Ibuki after she demands them. Grabs Ibuki when she tries to leave, then lets go and tells her to say goodbye. When Ibuki asks if he is satisfied with his time at school, he is silent; Ibuki leaves.

- **Spoken** (p.248) — “Kuku. I just got out of the bath, come in.”
- **Spoken** (p.248) — “Kuku, well aren’t you the smart one. Whatever, take em. They’re useless to me anyway.”

- **Spoken** (p.316) — “Kuku. It seems like the Grim Reaper has taken pity on me.”

- **Spoken** (p.348) — “Kuku. Who knows?”

### Year 1, Volume 11.5

#### p.194–214 — Ch.3: Return preparations and confrontation

In the Keyaki mall men’s toilet, Ryūen meets Hashimoto Masayoshi, who has called him there to discuss the year’s events. Hashimoto brings Kanzaki Ryūji to the meeting; Ryūen listens as Kanzaki criticizes his tactics in the end-of-year exam. After Kanzaki leaves, Ryūen reflects that he must be more careful while continuing to use ruthless methods. Ryūen meets Ayanokōji at Keyaki mall after receiving Hiyori’s message. He says he intends to enjoy the holidays longer before returning officially and claims he will destroy Ichinose and Sakayanagi as a warm-up. Ryūen suddenly attacks Ayanokōji but fails to land a decisive blow. After hearing Ayanokōji’s criticism of his laxative strategy, Ryūen accepts the advice while declaring he will defeat him one day.

- **Relationship · Hashimoto Masayoshi** (p.194–205) — Meets him and discusses future moves.
- **Relationship · Kanzaki Ryūji** (p.199–205) — Hears his warning about Class B.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.206–214) — Meets and clashes with him at the mall.
- **Relationship · Shiina Hiyori** (p.206) — Responds to the message she relayed from Ayanokōji.

#### p.194–213 — Depth review: the returning snake and the toilet confrontation

Meets Hashimoto in the second-floor men's toilet of Keyaki Mall, a secret, camera-free meeting.

- **Spoken** (p.195) — "Who knows. Maybe it's just a whim."
- **Spoken** (p.196) — "Don't think you can win just by wandering in the middle of the two sides as a double spy."
- **Spoken** (p.197) — "Ayanokouji? Ah, for a moment I thought it would be him."
- **Spoken** (p.198) — "Did you really call him over? I don't think you did."
- **Spoken · Kanzaki Ryūji** (p.201) — "I'm sorry Ryuuen, I don't think we lost to you."
- **Spoken** (p.207) — "You, who have defeated even me, can't be weaker than Sakayanagi."
- **Spoken** (p.208) — "Relax. The only person who can get you expelled is me."
- **Spoken** (p.209) — "My instincts are telling me that I should eat you alive."
- **Spoken** (p.211) — "Grow even stronger? Who are you, my teacher?"
- **Spoken** (p.212) — "I'll accept your garbage advice now. But one day I'll defeat you."
- **Spoken** (p.213) — "Next time, I'll destroy them even more perfectly and beautifully."
- **Trait** (p.194–213) — Returns with renewed spirit; accepts advice from Ayanokōji to grow stronger and reflects that his win over Class B was sloppy — not the method but being careless.
- **Relationship · Hashimoto Masayoshi** (p.194–200) — Mutual-interest talks; sees through his bluff about calling Ayanokōji.
- **Relationship · Kanzaki Ryūji** (p.199–205) — Confronted by him over the "dirty" tactics; respects his resolve and promises a harder fight.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.206–212) — Meets him after Hiyori's message; lashes out physically, then accepts his advice to grow.

#### p.381–398 — Short story: After spring break

In his short story perspective, Ryūen reflects near the end of spring break on his return and his desire to face Ayanokōji again. Sakayanagi approaches him at Keyaki mall and they exchange remarks about his comeback and their rivalry. Ishizaki and Ibuki meet Ryūen and report having seen Ayanokōji; Ishizaki mentions proposing that Ayanokōji join Class D. Ryūen tells them he intends to crush Class A this year while dismissing Class B. Ryūen meets Ayanokōji at Keyaki mall and confronts him over the message relayed through Hiyori. He declares his intention to target Ichinose and Sakayanagi after the break and attacks Ayanokōji but fails to land a hit. Ryūen listens to Ayanokōji’s criticism of his methods and resolves to improve while vowing to defeat him.

- **Relationship · Sakayanagi Arisu** (p.382–386) — Exchanges words with her at Keyaki mall.
- **Relationship · Ishizaki Daichi** (p.387–388) — Meets him and hears his report about Ayanokōji.
- **Relationship · Ibuki Mio** (p.387–388) — Meets with her and Ishizaki.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.381, 396–404) — Seeks a rematch with him and confronts him at the mall.
- **Relationship · Shiina Hiyori** (p.396) — Acts on the message she relayed.

#### p.90–117 — Day two: the pillow truce, the Gifu incident

Morning TV: kittens special; he makes a proposal to Kitō over the indigestion ("Let's have another game today."); he needles him with the magazine-sword; Kitō plays it deadly serious; Ryūen accepts the draw of a truce-ish morning. The bus is delayed; he watches the snowman building critically; Kushida needles him about the snowman invite; he finds it pathetic; he turns from the sight. Yamamura is bare-handed and shivering; he hauls her arm out of the pocket by force; she refuses; he proves there are no gloves on her; he orders Ayanokōji to lend her his; he calls it "clumsy kindness" his way; she wears them and keeps her distance afterward. Advanced course; dead-heat with Kitō on the steep slope; the two nearly collide; Ayanokōji inserts himself between them; both sides glare at him; Ryūen stomps off to the lift; wrestles with his own competitive streak. Soup curry lunch; he pulls Ayanokōji aside at the food court corner; he delivers the 800M plan (Sakayanagi must die by any means necessary; his class is being prepped; the plan is already in motion); he reminds Ayanokōji of last year's promise; he's genuine; then the Gifu confrontation. Five Gifu students gang up on Yamamura and Nishino; Yamamura apologized for the ramen-broth stain on his clothes; the boys won't accept it; Ryūen lands a knee kick to the stomach; Kitō appears behind them blocking escape; the boys beg on their knees in front of the girls; he forces them to swear; it's settled violently.

- **Spoken** (p.90) — “Hey, Kitō. You got a little indigestion from yesterday's pillow fight, didn't you? Let's have another game today.”
- **Spoken** (p.91) — “The skiing we're going to do sounds good, doesn't it?”
- **Spoken** (p.93) — “I thought that some checks and balances would make her quieter, but I guess I misread her.”
- **Spoken** (p.98) — “Hey, Yamamura, give me your hand.”
- **Spoken** (p.99) — “Well, that must be cold. Where are your gloves?”
- **Spoken** (p.99) — “You're probably not even good at skiing to begin with but you're not gonna wear gloves on top of that?”

### Year 2, Volume 9.5

#### p.113–134 — Ryūen: the summoned meeting on the third-term exam; the tailing reveal

He abruptly calls Ayanokōji and Katsuragi out to Keyaki Mall — ordering Ayanokōji to postpone his plans — and tears into a cake when they arrive, brushing off the suggestion that he apologize. He reveals he heard from third-years that a big, risky exam looms at the start of the third term; he wants a one-on-one shot at Sakayanagi in the final exam rather than a class vote, and parts telling Ayanokōji their match is in the third year. The stroll ends; Ryūen tells Ayanokōji "our match will be in the third year"; he waves off Ayanokōji's attempt to leave. He tails Katsuragi and Ayanokōji back; catches Yamamura watching; keeps her secret from Sakayanagi — partly out of pity, partly as a bear trap; teases Katsuragi for his schoolboy heart.

#### p.144–153, 156–161, 172–176, 180–184 — Ch.6: The hidden hand surfaces

The exam begins; Class C's lead attack lands on his class; he zones out staring at his phone for nearly a minute; classmates freeze; Hoshinomiya intervenes; his decision comes only at the deadline. First Category Lifestyle D1: Kitō, Kamuro, Hashimoto, Machida, Yamamura; Sakayanagi protects Machida; three answer right; A gets four points; D receives one. Fifth turn — he keeps targeting the same cluster (Kitō, Kamuro, Hashimoto, Yamamura, Machida, Sanada, Satonaka, Matoba); his class's defensive answer rate is dismal; they sit expressionless. Kaneda — the advisor — is the only one not even touching his phone; he's the only one working; Ishizaki sleeps at his seat. The cafeteria lunch; he crashes their table — wolf among sheep; plays the threat; Yamamura leaves the table? no; he reveals nothing — he leaves early with a message for AK: watch silently. He stands; brushes his hair; his face offers AK one glimpse of the beast behind the mask; the message is received. POV interiorization — the beast; the promise he made to himself; he slowly gets up before the second half begins. Turn 11 defense — Sakayanagi attacks with Sports D3; he responds by declaring five names without hesitation: Katsuragi, Shiina, Tokitō, Nomura, Ibuki; every single one answers or is protected; he calls it "gambling". Turns 12–14: he names the same five; four perfect protections in a row; the class is glued to the monitor; Sakayanagi has no answer. Ichinose's offer; his terms; the actual contract delivered through her phone — he takes what he wants.

#### p.172–199, 201–204 — Ch.8: The final stand

He enters forcibly; she offers him a seat; he crosses his legs; he tells her she's the one leaving on a new journey; they spar verbally about anti-heroes and who belongs in the leading role. He sees she excluded Hashimoto's group; she uses the traitor rights first — “Kuku, this as the first move...”; he commits to finding the traitor — triple-interrogation failure. He misses Tamiya — loses a life; Morishita double-hit — loses another; triple-interrogation failure against the hidden traitor — he discovers it only in the fourth round; discussion 1 ends 6-8 against him. Second discussion — he rides the Shimizu/Tokitō storm without nominating once; Shiina keeps Tokitō from self-destructing; both pass; both ID Asagaya — draw; no lives change. The grind continues; he recovers his first life (an underclassman); she dominates; fifth discussion — he hits Nishi, she hits Hoashi; he's down to one life; he declares defeat early. Ryūen tells her about AK's message — only Hashimoto would know it; she picks Hashimoto's group; he uses his traitor rights randomly; she goes to interrogation; he closes his eyes — the end is coming.

- **Spoken** (p.41) — “Don’t talk as if you can see through someone else’s mind. Are you Ayanokōji or what?”
- **Spoken** (p.41) — “What will you do after leaving here?”
- **Spoken** (p.41) — “She’ll be startled if you show up.”
- **Spoken** (p.42) — “Huh?”
- **Spoken** (p.42) — “...Next time, huh? You won’t have a next time, will you? Are you just making excuses?”
- **Spoken** (p.42) — “One year. Just one year, huh? Hah... bring it on.”
- **Relationship · Sakayanagi Arisu** (p.31–42) — He admits defeat; she advises him he cannot beat Ayanokōji alone and proposes to create a special weakness in Ayanokōji for him.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.33–42) — Ayanokōji's hidden message inverted the result; Ryūen resolves to defeat him within one year.

#### p.213–216 — Epilogue: waiting for Ayanokōji's father

In the first-year guidebook short story he comes to the school entrance out of curiosity about Ayanokōji's family and finds Horikita already waiting for the same reason. When asked, he tells Atsuomi plainly that he is neither classmate nor friend — just Ayanokōji's enemy — and calls him dangerous after two years of trouble. Atsuomi leaves; he calls the father's presence impressive but the meeting anticlimactic, then tells Horikita he will defeat both her and Ayanokōji.

- **Relationship · Horikita Suzune** (p.213–216) — They wait together and compare impressions of the man who appears; both feel something off about the explanation they hear.

### Year 3, Volume 3

#### p.86–89 — Ch.3 — depth read: Ryūen's caution after the ambush

Looks down at the shattered Class C students after the surprise attack.

- **Spoken** (p.87) — "No—send ten people."
- **Spoken** (p.88) — "No, this is as far as we go."
- **Spoken** (p.89) — "He held out longer than I expected."
- **Spoken** (p.89) — "The problem is we failed to take down the one guy we needed to."
- **Tactic** (p.87) — Sends a ten-person search party (with VIP Yamashita) so Identify Person can't cheaply reveal the VIP; calls off the pursuit because Ayanokōji survived.
- **Trait** (p.86–90) — Decisive and ruthlessly rational — struck first to deny Ayanokōji time to think, and values the psychological proof that Ayanokōji has openings more than the casualties.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.86–90) — Launched the ambush to defeat him before he could plan; regrets failing to take him out.
- **Relationship · Katsuragi Kōhei** (p.87–90) — Receives casualty reports and gives orders.
- **Relationship · Ishizaki Daichi** (p.88) — Holds him back from pursuing Class C.

#### p.86–90 — After the ambush: calling off the pursuit

Looks down at the shattered Class C students; the surprise attack has taken 15 of theirs including Kitou and VIP Nishikawa.

- **Spoken** (p.87) — "Yeah. If things go well, that's eighteen people. Not bad."
- **Spoken** (p.87) — "It's probably better if Kondou doesn't catch up."
- **Spoken** (p.87) — "No—send ten people."
- **Spoken** (p.88) — "I almost forgot myself, but moving in a small group will just give them a hint."
- **Spoken** (p.88) — "—No, this is as far as we go."
- **Spoken** (p.89) — "He held out longer than I expected."
- **Spoken** (p.89) — "This ain't about our losses. The problem is we failed to take down the one guy we needed to."
- **Spoken** (p.89) — "But as long as the most troublesome bastard is still standing, I wouldn't be surprised if he managed to turn the tables on us even when we've got twice the strength."
- **Spoken** (p.90) — "Now that one of their VIPs is down, Ayanokouji has no choice but to go on the offensive, like it or not. So there's no need for us to jump the gun."
- **Inference, not stated** (p.86) — The ambush was conceived at once on reading the rules and drawing the adjacent start — strike Ayanokōji before he has time to think, the best counter to his superhuman intellect.
- **Tactic** (p.87–88) — Raises the search party from the minimum to ten so Identify Person cannot cheaply reveal VIP Yamashita — one in ten odds.
- **Trait** (p.86–90) — Ruthlessly rational — values the psychological proof that Ayanokōji is not infallible more than the casualties, and refuses to press an advantage that could become a trap.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.86–90) — Launched the ambush to defeat him before he could plan; calls off pursuit because he survived.
- **Relationship · Katsuragi Kōhei** (p.87–90) — Receives casualty reports and debate, ultimately agrees to send the ten-person party.
- **Relationship · Ishizaki Daichi** (p.88–89) — Rebuffs his eager demand to chase Class C.

#### p.119–122 — Morning deliberation: whom to attack next

Wakes before 6 a.m. and studies the map, deciding whom to fight on day two.

- **Spoken** (p.120) — "I've got him cornered... but how're you gonna move, Ayanokouji?"
- **Spoken** (p.120) — "There's always the option of crushin' that eyesore Class A first... but that'd just make the guys at the bottom happy."
- **Spoken** (p.120) — "Well, the main target's always Class C."
- **Spoken** (p.120) — "Sure, maintainin' our numbers advantage ain't a bad thing. We're the ones holdin' the initiative here. But we already used our main tactic yesterday to get the first strike."
- **Spoken** (p.121) — "It's a strange thing. They should've been half-destroyed by that surprise attack, but lookin' at this map, I can't see Ayanokouji showin' any sign of weakness."
- **Spoken** (p.121) — "It's the fear of not being able to see your opponent's face. The stronger the enemy, the more one's imagination is stirred up."
- **Inference, not stated** (p.119) — Judges an all-out attack on Class C would win ~99% if the exam ended immediately, but would leave too few forces to beat A and D — rejects a war of attrition that benefits the bottom classes.
- **Trait** (p.119–122) — Obsessed with Ayanokōji and belligerent, but rational — recognizes the minuscule opening he found is hard to press and that time/hunger will force Class C's hand anyway.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.120–121) — Fixates on him as the main target even half-destroyed; cannot see any sign of weakness.
- **Relationship · Katsuragi Kōhei** (p.120–122) — Debates strategy with him; agrees ("Maybe so") to consider turning on Class A.

#### p.236–241 — Epilogue — depth read: the duel lost, defeat conceded

Emerges from the trees with Ibuki and Yamashita to confront Ayanokōji in the shrinking center of E11.

- **Spoken** (p.237) — "Yeah. But I'd like to hand you a loss just as much. Time's running out. Let's settle this here and now and decide our ranks."
- **Spoken** (p.241) — "So even you can't dodge every bullet here, can you?"
- **Spoken** (p.241) — "Gotta take every precaution when I'm up against you, Ayanokouji, right?"
- **Spoken** (p.241) — "Even in a 2-v-1, there's no guarantee we'd win a shootout against you."
- **Feat** (p.237–248) — Cornered Ayanokōji on the beach with an overwhelming ammunition advantage but lost when the damaged paintball burst on his abdomen; accepts the defeat with a declaration of future, merciless rivalry.
- **Trait** (p.241) — Obsessed with victory — chooses the 2-v-1 against Ayanokōji over the safer shot at a VIP because "settling things by running away on a rare occasion like this" is not fun.
- **Trait** (p.241–242) — Driven by obsession with a certain victory — refuses the safe all-or-nothing shot at a VIP and insists on beating Ayanokōji directly, even at the risk of his own defeat.
- **Relationship · Ayanokōji Kiyotaka** (p.236–250) — Fights him on the beach, concedes defeat, and vows no mercy from here on.
- **Relationship · Ibuki Mio** (p.241) — Says her preference was to gamble on an all-or-nothing shot at a VIP rather than challenge Ayanokōji.

Confronts Ayanokōji in the shrinking area with Ibuki and Yamashita.

- **Feat** (p.237–248) — Cornered Ayanokōji on the beach with overwhelming ammunition but lost to the thrown damaged paintball; accepts defeat with a declaration of future merciless rivalry.
- **Trait** (p.241–242) — Obsessed with a certain victory — refuses the safe VIP shot, insists on beating Ayanokōji directly, and demands to see how he can escape even at the risk of his own defeat.
